**The three checkable rows are machine-enforced as of 2026-08-18.**
`scripts/check_non_mf_proof_ledger.py` recomputes the blob hash, the sha256
and the line count on every run and fails when any of them disagrees with the
distributed file, so a manuscript edit that lands without a re-pin turns the

last in a wave still re-reads the rows the edit touched, then refreshes the
header with `--repin-header`; the refresh is deliberate and logged, never a
side effect of checking.

**No row is keyed on a line number.**  Every row is anchored either on a `\label`
or on a verbatim phrase from the claim itself that occurs exactly once in the
comment-stripped manuscript, and the checker re-locates each anchor on every run.
Insertions anywhere in the preamble or body move no row.  (Verified live: the file
grew by several hundred lines during this audit while the gate stayed green.)

**Concurrent editing.**  Ten sessions share this checkout and the manuscript is
rewritten while it is being read — during the 2026-08-17 pass the file took three
distinct blobs in the space of a few minutes.  Nothing below is reported as a
manuscript defect: **every row now carries `Edit = NO`**, and every discrepancy
the ledger records is a gap on the Lean side or a mismatch between the two, never
a claim that the TeX is wrong.

That is a change.  Until 2026-08-17 this paragraph named `RFS.03` as the single
`Edit = YES` row: a paragraph of `rem:finite-stage` had asserted that the
ultraproduct apparatus has no counterpart in the development, which two badged
declarations contradicted.  The paragraph is gone and so is the row, so the
exception no longer exists and the sentence recording it would now be false.
The general point it was making survives and is worth keeping: when the TeX says
something about the *development* rather than about mathematics, the ledger can
find it wrong, and no Lean work repairs it.

## Writing a step row: no pipes

A step row may not contain `\|`.  `split_row` understands the escape and always
has, but every *other* editor of this file — the agents and peer sessions that
rewrite rows in bulk, and the ad-hoc scripts they use — splits on `|` and silently
merges or shifts the cells.  That has broken the gate once already, on `RFS.02`,
where a claim quoting `\| S \|` came back with twelve cells.  The escape is now
rejected outright in the steps block, with the replacements named in the message:

| For | Write | Not |
| --- | --- | --- |
| a cardinality | `card S` | `\| S \|` |
| a norm | `‖x‖` (U+2016) | `\|x\|` |
| a word length | `len_S(x)` | `\|x\|_S` |
| a presentation bar | `⟨X ∣ R⟩` (U+2223) | `⟨X \| R⟩` |

Forty-five escaped pipes across eighteen rows were converted on 2026-08-16.  Three
of them were not cardinalities at all and had to be read rather than substituted:
`CO.05` was `Tr\|u−w\|²`, the trace of a square; `CO.07` and `CO.09` were the word
length `\|γ\|_S`; `CO.10` mixed all three in one cell.  A blanket replacement gets
those wrong, which is the other reason the escape is banned rather than rewritten
by tooling.

The manuscript was under active revision throughout this audit: it grew from
3854 to 4262 lines, Theorem `thm:signfree` (`E/⟨w⟩` is not MF) was added, the
soficity of `W/⟨ζ⟩` moved into a corollary and back into
Theorem `thm:commutinglamp`, Question 1 was rewritten from "is `E/⟨w⟩` MF?" to
"what is `Res_MF(E)`?", two new Section 12 endpoints appeared, and the badge
macro was reworked into margin notes.  Every row below was reconciled against
the revision named above, and the new material was audited on the same terms as
the rest.

`scripts/check_non_mf_proof_ledger.py` is what keeps the ledger attached to a
moving manuscript.  It re-derives the printed-statement digest of every anchored
environment and re-locates every prose probe, so a later revision cannot silently
invalidate rows — it names them.  When a printed statement really has been
reworded, re-read the rows on that anchor and then re-pin deliberately with
`--repin-digests`, which logs every digest it moves.  Prose probes are never
auto-repaired: a relocated paragraph is a claim that may have changed, and it
keeps failing until a human looks.

## What this document is, and why the existing gates are not enough

The repository already runs two synchronization gates over this manuscript:

* `scripts/check_non_mf_refs.py` — every `\leanverified{module}{decl}` badge resolves
  to a declaration that exists in the tree, in the named module;
* `scripts/check_non_mf_claim_manifest.py` — every numbered theorem-like environment
  occurs exactly once in `notes/NON_MF_NUMBERED_CLAIMS.json`, with a digest of its
  printed statement and at least one exact Lean mapping.

At the pinned blob the manuscript carries **111 `\leanverified` badge invocations
naming 109 distinct declarations, and 54 numbered claims** — counted from the
comment-stripped tex and from `notes/NON_MF_NUMBERED_CLAIMS.json`, not copied
forward.  The figures this paragraph used to quote, 94 and 55, were both wrong
by the time anyone read them, which is why they are now derived rather than
asserted; a reader who wants the current numbers should count them the same way
rather than trust this sentence.  Neither gate answers the question this ledger
exists to answer:

> Has every fact used in the *proof* of each printed assertion been formally
> established, and is every inferential step the **same step** as the one printed?

Badge resolution is not evidence.  `exact <someTheorem>` certifies that *a* true
theorem exists with the advertised outer proposition; it says nothing about whether
the Lean proof travelled the printed road, whether the objects are the printed
objects, or whether some hypothesis is quietly importing an unformalized theorem.
This ledger is the step-level answer.  It covers *all* substantive mathematical
assertions of the manuscript, not only the numbered environments: unnumbered
remarks, proof-bearing paragraphs, displayed constructions, and every inferential
step inside every printed proof.

## The grading standard

Four labels.  There is **no** "acceptable literature input" category.

| Label | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `EXACT` | Same assertion, same objects, same inference, fully Lean-proved. |
| `MISMATCH` | Lean proves it differently, substitutes an equivalent object, restricts the quantifier, reorders substantively, or strengthens/weakens so as to bypass the printed step. |
| `MISSING` | No complete Lean proof — **including** anything currently represented by an assumption, a conditional theorem, a literature interface, an axiom, a `sorry`, or supplied existence/reduction data. |
| `UNDER-SPECIFIED` | The printed text is a prose route with no determinate step content to bind; Lean supplies a proof the sentence merely gestures at.  `EXACT` would overstate what was checked, `MISMATCH` would blame Lean for the manuscript's compression. |

`UNDER-SPECIFIED` is the project-wide fourth mark introduced by
`notes/P13_STEP_AUDIT.md` and adopted here.  It is a claim about the *manuscript*,
not about Lean, and it is deliberately not a pass: a route sentence that no
determinate obligation can be read off is a place where "1:1" has no meaning yet.

The project is 1:1 with the manuscript only when **every** row is `EXACT` in both the
statement column and the proof column.  That is not the present state, and the
`MISMATCH`, `MISSING` and `UNDER-SPECIFIED` rows below are the correct output of the
audit, not a defect of it.

Two rules were applied uniformly, and they account for most of the non-`EXACT` rows:

1. **Quantifier scope is part of the assertion.**  Where the manuscript says "let `H`
   be a (countable) group" and the Lean statement reads `{H : Type}` — i.e. `Type 0`
   only — the Lean assertion is strictly narrower than the printed one and the row is
   `MISMATCH`, noted `univ0`.  The transfer "every countable group is isomorphic to
   one in `Type 0`, and the property is isomorphism-invariant" is true and routine,
   but it is not in the development, so it may not be assumed here.  Rows whose Lean
   statement really is universe-polymorphic (`∀ (G : Type u) …`) are not so marked.
2. **A step is `EXACT` only if a Lean declaration performing that step was read.**
   If the enclosing printed theorem is Lean-proved by a *different* decomposition, its
   individual printed steps are `MISMATCH` (the Lean proof does not go through them),
   even though the enclosing conclusion may itself be `EXACT`.  If the enclosing
   theorem is not Lean-proved at all, its steps are `MISSING`.

## Method

The TeX was read in full, in order.  For every claim the tree was searched starting
from the `\leanverified` badges, then `notes/NON_MF_NUMBERED_CLAIMS.json`,
`notes/NON_MF_CLAIM_DECLS.txt`, and direct grep.  Where a Lean declaration exists it
was opened and its proof read, together with the helper lemmas the proof delegates to,
to the depth needed to decide the proof-route column.  Declaration names in the `Lean`
column are written relative to the root namespace `GroupApproximation`, matching the
manuscript's own margin-note convention.

## Related audits, and what they overturned here

Three peer documents audit overlapping surfaces.  This ledger **cross-references
rather than duplicates** them: where one of them went deeper than this sweep did, its
verdict wins and the affected rows are marked `CORRECTED against …` in the `Note`
column.  Fifteen rows were corrected that way, all of them downgrades.

| Document | Surface | Relation to this ledger |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `notes/P13_STEP_AUDIT.md` | `sec:group`, `prop:literal-base-T`, `thm:p13-complete`, `rem:classical-base` | Overturns 8 rows: `BT.04` (split into `BT.04`–`BT.04d`), `BT.08`, `BT.08b`, `BT.09b`, `BT.13`, and `BT.19`–`BT.23b`.  Read it for the P13 material at full depth. |
| `notes/COLLAPSE_STEP_AUDIT.md` | `def:invwitness`, `thm:collapse` Steps 1–6, `rem:collapse-finite-stage` | Overturns 7 rows: `CO.04b`, `CO.07`, `CO.09`, `CO.09b`, `CO.11`, `CO.14`, `CO.14b`, `CO.14c`.  Read it for the collapse ladder at full depth, and for its findings S1–S8 about the manuscript. |
| `notes/NON_MF_CONDITIONAL_INVENTORY.md` | every badge's dependency surface | The authority on the `Deps` column: 7 of 100 badged declarations are mathematically conditional, 5 more carry a badge defect that is not conditionality.  Where its verdict and this ledger's `Deps` disagree, believe it. |

The correction pattern is worth naming, because it is the honest answer to "which rows
are least confident".  Every overturned row is one where I marked `EXACT` at a
granularity **coarser than the printed sentences** — one ledger row spanning four
printed assertions (`BT.04`), or a six-step ladder audited through module structure and
docstrings rather than through the 260-line proof body (`CO.*`).  Both peers read the
bodies and found real deviations.  Rows I audited at printed-sentence granularity, by
opening the proof, have held.

## Headline findings

1. **The printed proof of Theorem 3.1 is not the formalized proof.**  Section 3 prints
   an ultraproduct argument: the Hilbert-space ultraproduct `K_ω`, the norm
   ultraproduct `B_ω = ∏_ω B(K_n)`, faithfulness of its action, finiteness of `B_ω`
   (no proper isometries), a Kazhdan projection `P ∈ B_ω` obtained by continuous
   functional calculus at the isolated spectral point `1`, the one-sided compression
   `P ≤ Q = VPV*`, the isometry `σ = r + (1−Q)`, and `Q = P` by finiteness.  **The
   route is still the Appendix B one, but this finding is no longer "none of that is
   in the development" — updated 2026-08-16.**  Since it was written, `K_ω` has been
   built as a module (`UltraproductModelConstruction.Vec`, with the inner product
   deliberately omitted), `B_ω` has been realized as the `ℓ∞/c₀` corona along the
   cofinite filter with its finiteness proved (`kt_06_ultraproduct_finite`), the
   Kazhdan projection has been built in an abstract `C*`-algebra
   (`UltraproductKazhdanProjection`), and the `σ = r + (1−Q)` computation is
   `kt_10_isometry_identities`.  What is still absent is faithfulness of the action on
   `K_ω` (UF.02) and any ultraproduct along a genuine free `ω` (UF.01, UF.03); and
   Murray–von Neumann comparison is replaced throughout by unitary conjugacy (FC.01).
   **The clause about a genuine free `ω` is no longer true — updated 2026-08-18.**
   `OmegaAdjointCorona Y ω` *is* `∏_ω B(K_n)`: the quotient of the bounded sequences
   by the `ω`-null ideal, not by `c₀`.  `Sofic/OmegaWeightedAmbient.lean` builds the
   KT.01–KT.09 ambient over it — with the Kazhdan bundle, the representation
   `[Ad U_n(·)]_ω` and Dedekind finiteness all in place — and its
   `omega_route_kazhdan_transport` derives the conclusion of `thm:kazhdan-transport`
   through it, constructing the ambient from the theorem's own hypotheses rather than
   assuming it.  All of that was in the tree and **cited by nothing**;
   `Sofic/OmegaRouteManuscriptTransport.lean` states the result in the badged
   theorem's own shape, so the printed route and the printed statement now meet in one
   declaration, `manuscriptKazhdanTransport_omegaRoute`.  Two things are still true:
   the *badged* `manuscriptKazhdanTransport` continues to run through the cofinite
   corona, deliberately, so that a consumer's route is readable from the name it
   cites; and the `ω` route fixes the ambient group at `Type 0` while the badged form
   is polymorphic in it.  FC.01 was separately resolved on 2026-08-16 and the clause
   about unitary conjugacy is stale too — see that row.
   Lean's `KazhdanAsymptoticCommutant.transport` still runs the *Appendix B* proof:
   finite-stage spectral projection `cornerProjection = spectralAbove(hermitianAverage …) θ`,
   the robust-gap eigenvalue exclusion, spectral capture, and the equal-rank flip
   `KazhdanCornerMatrices.norm_one_sub_mul_flip` (`‖(1−p)q‖ ≤ ε/√(1−ε²)`).
   Consequently the finite-stage appendix is `EXACT` in all 18 of its rows, while of
   the 30 rows of Section 3's printed proof 16 are `MISSING` and 10 are `MISMATCH` —
   even though the theorem's *conclusion* is genuinely proved.
   **This finding is spent, and its heading with it — updated 2026-08-19.**  The
   sentence above is not merely stale, it is now inverted: all 30 `KT` rows are
   `EXACT` in both columns, so the printed proof of Theorem 3.1 *is* the formalized
   proof.  The last two cells to move were `KT.01`'s proof column, which was waiting
   on a green `Build and audit` to execute the two route guards `scripts/Audit.lean`
   pins on `manuscriptKazhdanTransport` — the printed opening and the printed choice
   of `ω` — and `ID.06`'s, which was waiting on the same run to certify
   `Sofic/PrintedReverseTransportRoute`.  Run `32217510998` on `dd1ca3546` supplied
   it.  What the finding described was real while it lasted; the rows below record
   route by route how each was closed, and the guards are what keep the closure from
   silently regressing to the Appendix B proof.
2. **The other analytic spines are route-faithful, with two audited exceptions.**  The
   finite-normal Reynolds corner, the normal-Kazhdan moving spectral subspace, the
   Appendix A tensor-power normalization (including the printed `8 < Nδ²` constant),
   the Appendix B error bookkeeping, and the soficity of `W` (including the `2|k|/N`
   wrap-around estimate) all follow the printed road.  **Withdrawn on peer evidence:**
   my earlier verdicts that the involutive collapse ladder and the property-`(T)`
   bridge follow it step for step.  `notes/COLLAPSE_STEP_AUDIT.md` finds nine deviations
   in the collapse — the sharpest being that Step 6 is re-derived inline and neither
   cited transport theorem is ever invoked — and `notes/P13_STEP_AUDIT.md` finds that
   the printed spectral localization of Step 1 is absent (a Neumann/Richardson
   iteration with a weaker constant stands in its place) and that the "circumcenter"
   of Step 3 is a minimal-norm point of a closed convex hull.  The printed constants
   `κ/64`, `1/64`, `3/64`, `1/8` do match.
3. **36 rows carry a literature dependency; about a dozen of those are used in a
   deduction rather than cited for scholarship.**  The load-bearing ones: exactness of
   `C*_red(W)` (GHW, KW1, KW2), amenable ⇒ MF (TWW, used for the sharpness of the
   Kazhdan hypothesis in Theorem C), the simple sofic envelope (ES05), Adian–Rabin
   (Rabin58), Neumann's continuum family (Ne), nonamenability of infinite Kazhdan
   groups and nonnuclearity of `C*_red` of a nonamenable group (BHV, Lance), and the
   general amenable-quotient case of Elek–Szabó (only the `ℤ` case is formalized).
   **Update 2026-08-17: three of those load-bearing entries are retired.**  Adian--Rabin
   is closed --- `D4'` supplies the undecidable source in coordinates, and `UN.02`/`UN.02b`
   are now `EXACT` and `unconditional`.  Neumann's family is exhibited rather than quoted
   (`UN.07`/`UN.07b`, same grade).  And amenable ⇒ MF left the *printed* surface when the
   sharpness paragraph was rerouted through the exhibited monomial models, so the print now
   states amenability only as an aside it explicitly does not use.  Still load-bearing:
   exactness, the simple sofic envelope, BHV/Lance, and Elek--Szabó beyond `ℤ`.
   The remaining literature rows are the "relation to prior work" attributions, which
   feed no deduction; their notes say so.  The manuscript declares the load-bearing
   ones itself; the ledger records all of them as `MISSING`, per the standard.
4. **Four rows are conditional by construction** — the torsion-free full-radical
   endpoints of Question 2 take `Nonempty (DefectRoutingData D)`, `ContinuumMultiplicity`
   takes Neumann's family as a quantified hypothesis, and Remark C.4's maximal-algebra
   endpoint is stated over a `StrictKazhdanCompression` datum.  **Corrected
   2026-08-17:** this used to end "All are labelled conditional in the TeX where
   the TeX mentions them", which was true of the first two and is no longer true
   of the routing endpoints — the TeX mentions them nowhere.  `Hull`,
   `FullMFRadicalEndpoint`, `DefectRoutingData` and `\leanconditional` each occur
   **zero** times in `non_mf_groups_exist.tex`, and there is no `\bibitem` for
   Hull.  The Question 2 item now asks the question and answers nothing, so the
   routing endpoints are conditional Lean that no printed sentence consumes.  See
   `notes/NON_MF_CONDITIONAL_INVENTORY.md` §3–4 for the full regrade; the
   distinction that matters is that the manuscript **stopped claiming** the
   conditional results rather than **acquiring** unconditional ones.
5. **One badge under-certifies its remark** — *possibly mid-edit, re-check.*  The
   badge macro was being rewritten into margin notes during this audit, so badge
   placement is exactly the kind of thing that may still be in flight.
   Remark C.4 (`rem:maxinfinite`) asserts
   that for a group with strict Kazhdan compression the maximal algebra is not stably
   finite, carries no faithful trace, and is neither RFD nor MF, with `E` as the running
   example.  Its badge names `QuasiRegularWitness.baseVector_apply_base`, which says
   only that the point mass at the base coset takes the value `1` there.  The
   substantive content does exist in `Analysis/MaximalCStarProperCompression.lean`, but
   it is stated over a `StrictKazhdanCompression` datum and is instantiated
   (`Sofic/ChosenMaximalCStarInfinite.lean`) for the *Chosen* presentation, not for the
   literal eight-generator `E` of Definition 7.1.  **Partly closed 2026-08-16:** the
   remark now carries a second badge,
   `MaximalCStarParagraphEndpoint.manuscriptMaximalCStarStrictCompressionRemark`,
   which certifies the paragraph's load-bearing conclusions in the printed sentence
   order (row MC.08b).  What remains open is the instantiation: MC.09 is still on the
   *Chosen* presentation and still states no RFD clause.
6. **Universe scope.**  30 of the 56 `MISMATCH` statements are `MISMATCH` for `univ0`
   alone — the Lean statement fixes `Type 0` where the manuscript quantifies over all
   (countable) groups.  These are the cheapest rows in the ledger to close: either make
   the statements universe-polymorphic, or prove the countable-group transfer once and
   route every printed statement through it.
   **This class is closed — updated 2026-08-19.**  Both routes named above were taken:
   the `_anyUniverse` wrappers state the printed sentence with no universe on the
   Kazhdan source, and the countable-group transfer they run through is
   `Type0Transfer.exists_type0_model`, with property (T) supplying the countability the
   print already states.  There are 9 `MISMATCH` statements left in the whole ledger,
   not 56, and none of them is a `univ0` row: they are `PRE.11`, `NK.07`, `CO.21`,
   `CO.21b`, `RE.05`, `SO.16`, `LI.16`, `LI.19` and `LI.19b`, each held for a reason its
   own note gives.  The "cheapest rows to close" sentence therefore no longer points at
   anything; what remains is not cheap.
7. **The material added during the audit is in good shape.**  Theorem `thm:signfree`
   (`E/⟨w⟩` is not MF, so `{1,w}` is a *proper* subgroup of `Res_MF(E)`), Corollary
   `cor:signfreesofic`, the dimension-free adjoint gap in Section 12, and the
   faithfully-traced coordinate no-go are all `EXACT` in both columns, step by step —
   including the parity argument that reduces the eight `(ℤ/2)³` conjugates of `d` to
   the marked word.
8. **The two ultraproduct lemmas that Section 3 factored out are over-badged**
   (2026-08-16).  `lem:ultrafinite` prints three assertions about `B_ω = ∏_ω B(K_n)`
   acting on `K_ω` — faithfulness, finiteness, and `ran P ⊆ ran Q ⟺ P ≤ Q` — and
   carries one badge, `kt_06_ultraproduct_finite`, which proves the middle one for a
   different object: the `ℓ∞/c₀` corona along the *cofinite* filter, as its own
   module docstring states.  `lem:finitecompare` prints Murray–von Neumann
   equivalence and is badged with a lemma about unitary conjugates, a strictly
   smaller class.  Rows UF.01, UF.03 and FC.01 were downgraded from `EXACT/EXACT`
   to `MISMATCH/MISMATCH` over substituted objects and UF.02 was added as `MISSING`.
   The substitutions are sound where the manuscript *uses* the lemmas; they are not
   sound for the lemmas as stated, which is what a badge on a numbered environment
   certifies.
9. **One printed sentence is now false about the development** (2026-08-16), and it
   is the only `Edit = YES` row in the ledger.  A paragraph added to
   `rem:finite-stage` says the ultraproduct apparatus "has no counterpart in the
   development, or in the library it is built on"; two of the four items it lists do

10. **The collapse remark grew a full quantitative route, and it holds up**
    (2026-08-16).  `rem:collapse-finite-stage` now prints the constants

## Corpus hygiene (verified)

`sorry`: 0.  `axiom`: 0.  `native_decide`: 0.  Checked across `GroupApproximation/`.

Those three greps are worth exactly what they measure and no more.  A corpus can
be free of all three and still contain declarations that have never been through
the kernel, which is the distinction the next section is about.

## Build state, and one clean instance of why it is a separate question

**The distinction this ledger keeps insisting on is: a name existing, a name
being cited, and a name having elaborated are three different facts.**  On
2026-08-17 CI supplied a textbook instance of the gap between the second and the
third.  A certificate run died at `Build and audit` on exactly one file,
`PrintedLiftingSteps`, for three `unusedSectionVars` — fatal because the node
compiles with `-DwarningAsError=true`.  Those three theorems existed in the tree,
`lean_decls` resolved them, a ledger row could have cited them and the ledger
gate would have passed, and **they did not elaborate**.  Nothing about them was
mathematically wrong; the build simply never accepted the file.

That is why rows here are held on closure membership and on green builds rather
than on `grep`, why the "Rows waiting for a first green build" section exists,
and why the orphan intersection described there is run rather than assumed.  It
is also why the marker is enforced by `check_steps` instead of being a note: a
convention that depends on remembering to apply it is a convention that fails on
the day someone is in a hurry.

**The tree does compile, and that is worth recording as precisely as the
failures.**  At `1bd2ab72`, `Build project` succeeded and `Build the audit
library` succeeded: 39041 declarations audited, `AXIOM 0`, `UNUSED 0`,
`LITERATURE_INPUT 0`.  That is the first clean compile-and-audit of 2026-08-17,
and it is the fact that makes the `EXACT` rows above mean anything at all — a
row graded against a declaration in a tree that does not build is a row graded
against nothing.  The signature regeneration that followed it is the other half:
`CLAIM_SIGNATURES.md` unchanged at 421 declarations, `NON_MF_CLAIM_SIGNATURES.md`
at 113, **42 insertions and zero deletions** across 58 changed Lean modules.
Zero deletions is the load-bearing number: nothing already mapped had its
signature *move*, so no mapped declaration silently changed its statement
underneath a badge, which is the defect regeneration exists to catch.

**A fourth thing, and it inverts the sign of the evidence: a green checkmark in
CI.**  `Source scan` and `Kernel audit` both carry `continue-on-error`, so the
GitHub UI and the API report them as conclusion `success` **even when they exit
1**.  The only place the truth appears is the Zero-tolerance verdict's `src=`
and `ker=` fields.  As of 2026-08-17 those read `src='failure'` — 15 orphan
modules at `main`, 18 at the certificate's tree, 0 at `verified` — and
`ker='failure'`, with `RFL 17` and `DUPLICATE 9`.  A reader trusting the green
ticks would conclude the opposite of the truth.  This is the same shape as
everything else in this section, one layer further out: the gate ran, the gate
failed, and the reporting surface says it passed.

**A third thing that is not the same as the other two: what a commit message
says.**  On 2026-08-17 a commit subject read "htransport discharged".  Checked
against the file, the bridge it built is real and is the printed inference, but
it sits *beside* the endpoint rather than inside it — `collapse_contradiction_of_hasKazhdanPropertyT`
still takes `htransport` as a binder — and its two modules are outside the root
import closure.  So CO.21b records the advance and does not take the grade.
Reading the commit subject instead of the file would have moved a `MISMATCH` to
`EXACT` on the strength of a sentence written by someone describing their own
work in good faith.

**The same distinction bit the person running the tooling, which is the useful
part.**  The first signature regeneration of 2026-08-17 failed reporting that a
mapped declaration was "not in the environment" — which reads exactly like a
missing theorem, and was not one.  The build the script's docstring requires had
been skipped, so the environment was stale: the declaration existed, and the
environment being queried had not elaborated it.  Nobody was careless and the
error message was accurate; it was accurate about a different question than the
one it appeared to answer.  Anyone reading a "not in the environment" failure
should check what built the environment before concluding anything about the
tree.

## A failure mode no gate here can see: two claimed proofs collapsing into one

Every check this repository runs is *local to one claim*: a badge resolves, a
statement digests, an anchor locates, a declaration exists, a row cites.  On
2026-08-17 a proposed remediation would have broken something none of them look
at — a relation *between* two claims — and every gate would have stayed green.

The manuscript announces **two independent proofs** of `thm:kazhdan-transport`:
the Section 3 ultraproduct argument, and the quantitative finite-stage argument
of `app:finite-stage`, with `rem:finite-stage` printed precisely to tell the
reader they are different.  The proposed fix for `FN.03` and `ID.05` was to
repoint `KazhdanAsymptoticCommutant.transport` onto the printed ultraproduct
route.  But `transport` is not a consumer of the finite-stage proof, it is the
**engine underneath it**: `ManuscriptKazhdanTransport.finiteStageKazhdanTransport`
calls it, and the `cor:generaltransport` badge chain bottoms out there too.
Repointing it would have left the corpus holding **one proof under two names**,
with `finiteStageKazhdanTransport` being the ultraproduct argument wearing the
finite-stage label, while the manuscript went on claiming two.

Nothing would have flagged it.  No badge moves, no printed statement changes, no
anchor relocates, no declaration disappears, no row's `Lean` cell stops
resolving.  A text scan sees two names; a digest sees two unchanged statements;
this ledger sees two rows, each citing something real.  The lane asked to do it
refused and built a parallel literal-route declaration instead, leaving the
finite-stage engine untouched — the catch was a person reading an import graph,
not a gate.

**The shape of the gate that would catch it**, if one is ever built: for every
manuscript sentence asserting that two proofs are independent, the two Lean
routes must not share a load-bearing declaration — computable by taking the
transitive dependency sets of the two endpoint declarations and requiring their
intersection to contain nothing either route could not do without.  Until such a
gate exists, the rule is procedural: **a remediation that repoints an existing
declaration must be checked against what depends on it, not only against what it
proves.**  This is the badge-strength defect class one level up — not a badge
citing a true theorem that is not the printed one, but two badges citing one
theorem where the print claims two.

## Provenance of the git history itself

Two incidents on 2026-08-17 are worth recording, because a reader reconstructing
this work from `git log` would otherwise be misled by both.

**Four merge resolutions took one side of this file wholesale**, reverting
finished regrade sets that the checker cannot see are missing — a reverted note
breaks no anchor.  Three were caught by re-running the row census and noticing
the count move; the fourth was caught before landing.  The resolution rule
adopted after them is that a conflict in this file is settled by taking *both*
sides' rows, never one side, because conflicts here are almost always disjoint
row sets — each lane edits its own IDs.

**One commit's message was overwritten** by a `git commit --amend` that caught a
moved `HEAD`: the content was untouched, the description replaced.  It was
restored forward in an empty commit, `7ab8bd96`, rather than by rewriting
history.  So one commit in the log carries a subject describing different work,
and `7ab8bd96` is where its real description lives.

## Rows waiting for a first green build

`scripts/lean_decls.py` answers "does this name exist in the tree", and the gate has
always taken that as the standard for the `Lean` column.  It is not evidence that a
declaration is *proved*: a module can be committed, imported, and named here while
having never once compiled.  There is no local build tree in this checkout — the
builds run on the cluster — so nothing here can tell the two apart by inspection.

Rows in that position carry `PENDING FIRST GREEN` in their notes.  The marker is
enforced, not decorative: `check_steps` rejects any row that carries it and is `EXACT`
in either column, so a proposed upgrade cannot be banked as a completed one while it
waits.  Each such note records the declarations proposed, who proposed them, and what
they would establish; when the build goes green, drop the marker and grade.

**A second way into this state, found 2026-08-17 and worth guarding against.**
The rows above got here by proposing declarations from a module known to be
unbuilt.  `MC.04` got here without anyone proposing anything: it cited three
declarations under one namespace, and two of them turned out to live in an
**orphan** module -- not merely unbuilt, but outside the root import closure, so
`lake build` cannot reach it even in principle.  A namespace shared between a
compiled file and an orphan file makes the citation look settled, and
`lean_decls` confirms the name exists, which is the very inference this section
exists to block.  The check that finds it is mechanical and takes a moment:
resolve each name in a `Lean` column through `lean_decls.build_index`, map the
location back to a module, and intersect with `check.orphan_paths`.  Run over
the whole ledger it returned exactly the two names above, so this is a spot fix
rather than a wave -- but the intersection is empty only until the next module
lands without its root import.

| Row | Currently | Proposed once green |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MC.04 | CLEARED 2026-08-18 | EXACT/EXACT. Condition met by run 32122738761 on `c0f1e4e8`: `Build and audit` `completed/success`, `Zero-tolerance verdict` green, `602077dd` an ancestor |
| CO.17 | RETIRED 2026-08-18 | Nothing is waiting: THIS ROW HAS NO STEP ROW. `CO.17` occurs in this file three times -- twice in prose and once here -- and never as an entry in the step table, in any of the last forty commits to touch the ledger. The reason is not a lost row but a cut sentence: the claim was the Step 2 cube identity behind `WreathWitnessW3.pauli_rank_identity_fails`, and the manuscript now contains the string `Pauli` zero times, so there is no printed sentence left to grade. The declaration is still in the tree and still true; it simply certifies nothing that is printed. Kept visible rather than deleted, because the prose below counts this row |
| SO.17 | CLEARED 2026-08-18 | EXACT/EXACT on `SimpleSoficEnvelopeUnconditional.manuscript_simpleSoficEnvelope`, with `ElekSzaboHyper` out of `Source`. Both named modules are imported at `c0f1e4e8`, the commit the certificate covers |

**The waiting table has no live entries as of 2026-08-18.**  `MC.04` and
`SO.17` cleared on the first green `Build and audit` (run 32122738761 on
`c0f1e4e8`, since completed `success` in every job), and `CO.17` is retired
above for a different reason entirely -- its printed sentence is gone, so the
row it was waiting to upgrade does not exist.

**A gate blindspot found while clearing it, left for whoever wants it.**
`scripts/check_non_mf_proof_ledger.py` validates the step table, the anchor
table and the counts block against each other, but it does not check that a
`Row` named in the waiting table is the id of an actual step.  `CO.17` sat here
naming a step that had not existed for at least forty ledger commits, and every
gate stayed green.  The check is one set difference and would have caught it the
day the row was cut.

**Six of the eight cleared on 2026-08-17: CY.04, CY.11, CY.12, CY.13, CO.02 and
CO.14c**, leaving CO.17 and MC.04 above.  **MC.04 was cleared with them and has
been restored, correctly, by a peer against the reading of the auditor who
cleared it.**  The distinction the restoration turns on is real and I had blurred
it: the four `CY`/`CO` modules entered the closure at `bf6a0b8a` on 2026-08-16,
so a root build cycle had had the opportunity to elaborate them, whereas
`Analysis/ProperIsometryStrictOrder` reached the closure only today and the
certificate that would first contain it is the one still running.  "In the
closure, therefore elaborated" needs a build to have happened in between, and
for this row none had.  I had also taken a green report at second hand for it,
which is the third of the three things this ledger keeps separate — see the
build-state section — and I should have applied that to my own row first.
CY.10 left the table by a different door again: its claim became a ghost when
the sharpness paragraph was rewritten, so there was no pending grade left to
wait for.
What changed for four of them is not a report of a green run — none is observable from
this checkout — but the condition the marker was actually waiting on.  When it was
written, the modules those rows name had been committed without being reachable from
`GroupApproximation.lean` at all, so no build could have elaborated them however green
it went.  Commit `bf6a0b8a` (2026-08-16) wired them into the closure, verified by a
transitive scan.  A module in the closure is elaborated by the root build or the root
build is red; that is a different and much stronger position than a module the build
never sees.

**CY.13 cleared for a stronger reason: it stopped needing the binder.**
`Sofic/CliffordBSAmenableMF.lean`, which the old note described as not compiling and

printed relators, proving no MF statement about any group and taking no hypothesis, so
the row is `unconditional` — with the proof column `MISMATCH`, because that is not the
route the paragraph prints.  See the row.

The two rows left above are held for a different reason each, recorded in their notes.

**Nineteen modules are outside the closure as of 2026-08-17**, up from one, so the
argument above has to be re-run per module rather than assumed.  Rows whose

declarations instead) and `Sofic/CollapseRankWeightTransport` (CO.14c).

**The 2026-08-16 retraction of CY.11 and CY.12 stands as a lesson and is not being
repeated.**  That upgrade was made on declaration existence alone.  This one was made
after reading each cited statement in full and checking its binders — in CY.12's case
specifically checking that neither cited declaration mentions MF or takes the `tww`
binder, so that the TWW input cannot enter through a row that is only about amenability.

**One premise, and now one row.**  The Tikuisis--White--Winter input is a single
exposed binder `tww : ∀ G, IsAmenable G → IsOperatorMF G`, appearing once, in
`CliffordBSPrintedRoute.isOperatorMF_realizedQuotient`.  It used to be the premise of
three rows; as of 2026-08-17 it is the premise of **one**, CY.10, and `INT.11` remains
its `literature-input` row.  CY.12 never needed it — that row is the amenability half
of its sentence, and both declarations it names mention neither MF nor the binder — and
CY.13 stopped needing it, because `Sofic/CliffordBSAmenableMF.lean` is now in the root
import closure and reaches the paragraph's conclusion with no hypothesis at all.

The binder is still not dischargeable, and CY.10 still carries it: the paragraph's
*printed* inference is amenable ⟹ MF, and `Analysis.AmenableQuasidiagonal` shows why no
substitute is available for it here — permutation operator-norm models certify exactly
the LEF groups, and `Monsters.CyclicBaseLEFObstruction` proves the realized quotient is
not LEF.  What `CliffordBSAmenableMF` supplies is a different road to the same place,
which closes the conclusion without closing the printed step.

| `thm:abstract-nk` | **2026-08-18: proved first and in full** — the Kazhdan-corner construction moved here verbatim from the proof of `thm:normal-kazhdan`, whose printed proof is now the two-line derivation with D = N_conj | NK.03–NK.09, NK.13 re-anchored to `thm:abstract-nk`; NK.02 grades the derivation; both statements unchanged |

Four further anchors — `thm:exactfd`, `thm:cyclic`, `thm:reduced`, `thm:witnesssofic` —
were renamed from the letter spellings `thm:B`, `thm:C`, `thm:D`, `thm:E` when the
manuscript made the descriptive labels primary.  See "The alias-label blindspot".

### Second wave, later on 2026-08-16: a rename pass

Twenty-five more anchors moved a few hours later, and the diffs were read the same
way.  **All twenty-five are one terminology pass; no step content changed**, so no row
was regraded on their account:

| The manuscript renamed | to |
| --- | --- |
| the MF radical, `Res_MF(G)`, `Rad_fd(G)` | the MF residual, `Res_MF(G)`, `Res_fd(G)` |
| `N_comp` | `N_conj` |
| `G_comp(L)` | `G^{+}(L)` |
| the compressed copy of `L` | the conjugate copy of `L` |
| involutive compression witness | involutive orbit witness |
| the capture term `c` | `c_cap`, bounding the loss in squared Frobenius norm |
| the marked word / marked commutator | the distinguished word / distinguished commutator |

Three of the twenty-five also gained or tightened prose, and each was checked against
its rows: `def:radical` added the sentence justifying "residual" (it is the smallest
normal subgroup with MF quotient) — a new claim, but one RA.01 and the functoriality
rows already cover; `rem:collapse-finite-stage` was tightened without losing a step,
and CO.16's claim follows the `c_cap` rename; `cor:undecidable` restated its reduction
with an explicit computable map `e`, which is the same hypothesis spelled out.

### Third wave, 2026-08-17: the sentence-level pass (commit `36490e17`)

A user-approved pass of 184 sentence-level edits landed across every section.
Twenty-one printed statements moved and twenty-four prose probes stopped
occurring — the largest single drift the ledger has taken.  All forty-five were
read before anything was re-pinned, and this is the record of that reading.

**Prose probes.**  All twenty-four were repointed by hand onto the sentence the
paragraph now prints, never deleted, and each new probe was checked to occur
exactly once.  Three of the twenty-four (`p:prelim-notation`,
`p:transport-display`, `p:B-why-corona`) had been reported by a peer's gate run
as anchors matching nothing at all; they are repaired here on the same terms as
the rest.  Nine probes had been anchored on a project-specific noun that the
pass renamed, which is the failure mode the section below already names.

**Statements.**  Four of the twenty-one changed content and their rows changed
with them; the other seventeen were wording.

| Anchor | What moved | Verdict on the rows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cor:undecidable` | **content**: the first assertion is now unconditional — "Then `W` is undecidable, and no algorithm decides…"; only the negative-side clause keeps its `if the complement of W is not recursively enumerable` premise | UN.02 **upgraded**, UN.03 restated; see those rows |
| `prop:proper-isometry` | **content**: the two absorbing hypotheses are replaced by the single strict domination `p < upu*` | MC.04 **restated** on the new hypothesis |
| `thm:markedclosed` | "finite-radius certificate" → "finite-radius obstruction" | ML.04's claim restated in the printed word; no step content moved, and the declaration is unchanged |
| `def:invisible` | **content**: `K₂^ω(U)` is now identified as the kernel of the induced homomorphism into the tracial ultraproduct, and hence normal | CD.05, CD.06 re-read; the normality clause is new print and is recorded |
| `thm:A` | the derivation sentence "the defining relations make `w` central, and with `c²=1` centrality forces `w²=1`" is compressed to "the element `w` is central and satisfies `w²=1`" | wording; TA.00 stands, and the compressed sentence is the *conclusion* of what it replaced |
| `prop:maximal-cstar` | the universe-management clause deleted | wording; MC.02's note already audits the universe of the target |
| `rem:maxinfinite` | the closing contrast with `Cred(E)` rephrased | wording; MC.07, MC.08, MC.08b stand |
| `rem:ff-realization` | "contains a copy of every" → "contains every" | wording; KC.21--KC.23 stand |
| `def:radical` | the "residual" justification now also says the quotient is MF, citing `prop:univquot` | wording; RA.01 and the functoriality rows cover it |
| `lem:unitarycorona`, `def:pattern`, `cor:generaltransport`, `thm:kazhdan-clifford`, `thm:exactfd`, `def:E`, `fig:compression-pattern`, `rem:classical-base`, `con:clifford`, `thm:reduced`, `thm:Esofic`, `thm:signfree` | wording only | rows stand |

**One thing the digest cannot see, and it is the largest change of the day.**
A statement digest fingerprints the *statement*, not the proof, so a deleted
proof leaves every digest on its anchor intact.  Commit `3a45fa60` deleted the
printed three-step property-`(T)` proof of `prop:literal-base-T`, and the
anchor's digest never moved.  See "Deleted proofs are invisible to the gate"
below.

### Fourth wave, 2026-08-19: the external literature-review pass

An external referee-style review of the manuscript raised six documentation
items against the printed text (numbered 4--9 in that review).  The pass that
answered them touched proofs and free prose almost everywhere and moved exactly
one printed statement.

| Anchor | What moved | Verdict on the rows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `rem:ff-realization` | **content of the attribution, not of the mathematics**: the remark cited "personal communication, 2026"; Fournier-Facio's paper is now public and states the input, so the remark cites `\cite[\S2]{FFF}` and prints the paper's own form `P₁ × P₂ × S ≤ P`, noting that the third factor is unused here | KC.21 re-read and its note updated -- the input is no longer unpublished, and the row stays `MISSING`/`literature-input` because it is still a cited theorem with no formal counterpart.  KC.22 and KC.23 stand: their printed sentences are untouched |

Four changes landed in proofs and free prose, where no digest reaches:

* `cor:undecidable`'s proof now cites `\cite{AanderaaCohen}` for the modular
  machine it takes as its source (the reference was invoked by name and absent
  from the bibliography), and states the **effective** Adian--Rabin form it
  actually uses -- a computable map of presentations with the two clauses --
  citing `\cite{Rabin58,Adian55}` and the translation `\cite{NybergBrodda}`.
  **One printed clause was wrong and is corrected**: the proof said the collapse
  case yields the trivial group, which is the classical construction, whereas
  the construction formalized here and badged on this proof
  (`AdianRabinVariantTransform`) yields a *free* group -- see
  `RabinVariantMF.pres_isOperatorMF`, which routes through
  `isOperatorMF_of_residuallyFinite` on `FreeGroup`.  Both are MF, so no row's
  conclusion moves; UN.04's claim ("presenting an MF group exactly when the
  input word is trivial") was already stated at the level the Lean proves it.
* The soficity proof's transitivity sentence (`sec:Esofic`) is expanded from one
  clause to the mechanism: `Γ̄/ᾱ(Γ̄) ≅ (ℤ/2)³` by translation parity, left
  multiplication factoring through the reduction `L̄ ≤ GL₃(𝔽₂)` of the rotation
  subgroup, and the seven words `1, x, x², z, yx, y²x, xyx` carrying `ē₁` to the
  seven nonzero classes.  This is what
  `LiteralAffineCosetTransitivity.conjD_cosetTransitive` proves, and printing it
  matters for a second reason: the reviewer's suggested gloss -- "the linear
  `SL₃(𝔽₂)`-part acts transitively on the seven nonzero vectors" -- would route
  the step through `\cite[Theorem~2]{CRW}`, the identification of the linear
  part with `SL₃(ℤ)` that `rem:classical-base` prints and that
  `LiteralAffineCosetTransitivity` deliberately avoids.  The printed sentence
  now records that this step is independent of that input; the identification
  itself stays where it is, in `rem:classical-base`, which does cite CRW.
* The `Exactness` paragraph no longer claims Dykema's theorem "for free products
  of arbitrary families".  It now exhibits the chain `Qₙ` of free products of the
  first `n` blocks -- finite factors, so each `Qₙ` is exact by Dykema -- and
  concludes with the increasing-union criterion, which is
  `\cite[Lemma~2.5]{KWExact}` and **not** `KWPermanence`: the union result is
  stated in the earlier Math. Ann. paper and only quoted in the Doc. Math. one
  ("In [KW, Lemma 2.5] we showed that a group which has an increasing family of
  exact open subgroups with union the whole group is itself exact"), which is
  why the citation moved.  SO.16 is unaffected -- it is a `MISMATCH` on the
  crossover link, which this does not touch.
* `\cite{ShulmanTraces}` and `\cite{FFF}` are now version-pinned in the
  bibliography (v5, 31 July 2026; v2, 14 August 2026).  The trace definitions in
  Shulman's source have moved between versions; the printed sentence "the
  approximating maps themselves need not be linear, positive, unital, or
  completely positive" was checked against v5 and is correct there, the three
  defects being asymptotic in the stated norm.

Nothing outside the manuscript's bibliography and these passages moved, and no
prose probe stopped occurring.

### Prose probes: what survives an editing pass, and what does not

Sixteen probes went to zero occurrences across the day, in three waves.  The lesson is
worth encoding, because the first repair guessed wrong.

*Wrong guess:* prefer notation-heavy fragments, on the theory that prose passes rewrite
English and leave mathematics alone.  That held for the vocabulary pass and then failed
completely for the rename pass, which renamed the notation itself — `Res_MF` and
`N_comp` were exactly the tokens the probes had been anchored on.

*What actually survived every pass:* cross-references (`\ref{thm:signfree}`),
numerals and named constants, and ordinary connective prose containing no
project-specific noun.  A probe should be the part of a sentence that would read the
same under any renaming of the objects it is about — "identifies the entire
conjugation orbit", "supply the memberships this iteration consumes", "For a stable
group the two".  Short is better than distinctive: every extra noun is another chance
to be renamed.

## The alias-label blindspot

When the manuscript made `\label{thm:exactfd}` primary and kept `\label{thm:B}` beside
it as a legacy alias, the four lettered anchors reported

> `\label{thm:B}` is not inside a labelled environment

which is false on its face — the label is inside `\begin{theorem} … \end{theorem}` —
and, worse, was unfixable through the tool the message points at:
`environment_digests` mapped only each environment's **first** label, so `repin` looked
`thm:B` up in the same map, found nothing, skipped the row, and printed
`repinned 0 anchor digest(s)`.  A gate that says "re-pin with `--repin-digests`" while
that flag silently declines to act is a gate that trains its readers to route around
it.  `scripts/check_non_mf_proof_ledger.py` now maps **every** label of a labelled
environment to that environment's digest, and reports an anchor keyed on a
non-primary label as exactly that, naming the primary label to use.  Both behaviours
are covered by `--self-test`.

**Closed.**  The aliases are gone.  Every `\ref` that named one now names the
primary label — `thm:exactfd`, `thm:cyclic`, `thm:reduced` — and `thm:E` had
already fallen to zero uses.  The hazard this section described, a *moved* alias
silently retargeting every `\ref` to the wrong theorem while the build stays
green, went with them, so the gate needs no rows to guard it and the `MISSING`
counts stay meaningful.

The rule that replaces the paragraph is in the manuscript's own comment at
`thm:exactfd`: **label after the content, never after the expected letter.**
`mainthm` numbers by position, so a label spelled `thm:X` is a promise about
position that the source cannot keep — `thm:D` had come to sit on the theorem
that prints as Theorem C, and needed a comment above it saying so.

## Anchors

`env` anchors are labelled environments; the digest is the SHA-256 of the printed
statement, normalized exactly as in `scripts/check_non_mf_claim_manifest.py`
(environment delimiters, `\label`, and `\lean…` badges removed, whitespace collapsed),
truncated to 16 hex characters.  `sec` and `eq` anchors are labels only.  `prose`
anchors are unlabelled passages, located by a verbatim probe that must occur exactly
once in the comment-stripped manuscript; the probe is the drift detector.

## Steps

Column key.  `Stmt` = statement status; `Proof` = proof-route status; `Obj` = object
identity (`literal` / `substituted`); `Deps` = dependency status (`unconditional` /
`conditional-data` / `literature-input` / `open`); `Source` = external source the TeX
cites for the step, `-` if none; `Edit` = whether a TeX edit is permitted (`NO`

| TV.01 | thm:transport-variants | transport at an arbitrary nonnegative weight sequence w_n, both directions | `TransportVariantsAnyUniverse.scaled_transport_both_anyUniverse`; `ScaledKazhdanTransport.scaled_transport_both` | EXACT | EXACT | literal | unconditional | - | NO | `IsScaledAsymptoticCommutant`/`IsScaledMassBounded` are the printed Tr-conditions verbatim.  **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 from MISMATCH, by wrapper rather than by sweep.**  The two measurements below stay on record because they are why the sweep is not owed, rather than merely unfinished.  `KazhdanCompressionCore` no longer pins its source group — it is `(Γ : Type v)` with `kazhdan : HasKazhdanPropertyT.{v, v} Γ`, green across all forty-four files that use it — so the printed generality can now at least be *written*.  Generalizing the transport theorems' own binders does not come free, and the two attempts say where the wall is.  (i) A `whnf` heartbeat timeout appeared in `scaled_laplacian_massVanishing` with fifteen cascade errors after it; the cause was **not** that declaration but `ScaledMassVanishing.finset_sum`'s own `{I : Type}`, one pinned binder in a helper — the same misleading shape as a structure whose projections all come back unknown.  (ii) With that widened to `{I : Type*}` the timeout is gone and the real wall appears: `KazhdanCompressorCorner.cornerProjection` and its neighbours pin `IsKazhdanPair.{0, 0}`, so the cascade continues into the corner layer and reaches the representation universe, not just the group's.  Both attempts were reverted, not left half-applied.  Since `thm:transport-variants` inherits its hypotheses from `thm:kazhdan-transport`, which grants no countability, the honest closure is the derived-countability transfer that closed KT.00 rather than a sweep: `C.kazhdan` is already `HasKazhdanPropertyT.{v, v} Γ`, which is exactly what `exists_symmetric_generating_finset` consumes, and `HasKazhdanPropertyT.of_mulEquiv` already exists.  The last missing ingredient now exists: `CompressionUniverseTransfer.mapCoreSource` transports a compression core along an isomorphism of its *source* (`mapCore` moves the ambient), and `KazhdanCompressionCore.countable_source` derives `Countable Γ` from the core's own `kazhdan` field.  Property `(T)` needs three moves there and all three were already in `Kazhdan/`: `liftUniverse` raises the representation universe to `max w v` so it can see both group universes, `of_mulEquiv` carries the property across, and `of_max` brings it back down to the target's.  Both of those remaining pieces are now done, in `Sofic/TransportVariantsAnyUniverse.lean`.  The predicates carry their own universe binder: `IsScaledAsymptoticCommutantOf` already did, and `IsScaledAsymptoticIntertwinerOf` was generalized to `{G : Type v}`, every existing use instantiating `v := 0`.  The wrappers are `TransportVariantsAnyUniverse.scaled_transport_both_anyUniverse`, `manuscriptIntertwinerTransport_anyUniverse` and `scaled_intertwiner_transport_anyUniverse`.  Since `mapCoreSource` fixes `t`, the compressed sequences in the conclusion are the same terms at the `Type 0` model as they are at `Γ`, so only the homomorphism has to be read back along the isomorphism; `commutantOf_of_precomp` and `intertwinerOf_of_precomp` are that single step, one per predicate.  **This is not a weakening**: the print grants no countability and none is assumed, because property `(T)` implies it. |

| NK.05 |thm:abstract-nk |the classes π(g) = [V_{g,n}]_ω define a unitary representation on the Hilbert ultraproduct; for h = card(S)⁻¹ Σ_{a∈S} π(a) the Kazhdan pair puts sp(h) in [−1,θ] ∪ {1} with θ = 1 − ε₀²/(4·card S) | `VectorOmegaAction.OmegaUnitaryRep.rep'`; `VectorOmegaKazhdanGap.manuscriptNormalKazhdanSpectralGap` | EXACT | EXACT | literal |unconditional |- |NO | **CLOSED 2026-08-18, and the objects are now the printed ones.**  The old note was accurate and is worth keeping: the printed cut is the spectral projection of h in the norm ultraproduct, and the declaration cited before performed the same cut at a finite stage.  The reason it performed the cut at a finite stage is that the manuscript's ambient did not exist in the development.  `Sofic/KOmegaHilbert.lean` builds `K_omega`, the ultraproduct of the MATRIX spaces under a renormalized Hilbert-Schmidt inner product, whose ambient is the corona on the DOUBLED index because `B(K_n)` is `M_{d_n^2}`; this row is about the ultraproduct of the coordinate spaces `C^{d_n}` themselves, whose ambient is the corona on the undoubled index.  Four modules supply it: `Analysis/VectorHilbertUltraproduct.lean` builds `H_omega` with its inner product, `Analysis/VectorHilbertComplete.lean` makes it complete and hence a Hilbert space, `Analysis/VectorOmegaAction.lean` makes `B_omega` act on it, and `Analysis/VectorOmegaKazhdanGap.lean` applies the gap.  The first two were written but were OUTSIDE THE ROOT IMPORT CLOSURE and did not elaborate; the last two are new.  **The hypothesis is the manuscript's, not a stronger one.**  `OmegaUnitaryRep` asks only that the coordinate multiplication defects be null ALONG the ultrafilter, which is what an operator-norm asymptotic representation supplies; the representatives need not multiply at any stage and `V_{1,n}` need not be the identity.  Triviality at the unit is proved, not assumed: the class of the unit is idempotent by the defect at the pair (1,1) and injective because it is an isometry.  **The conclusion is the printed inclusion, not the estimate.**  `manuscriptNormalKazhdanSpectralGap` reads `spectrum R h` is contained in `Icc (-1) (1 - eps^2/(4 * card S))` union `{1}`, with the manuscript's own theta; the lower endpoint is the bound `norm h <= 1` because h averages unitaries, and the upper one is the Kazhdan gap.  ROUTE: the printed sentence asserts the inclusion and derives nothing, so there is no printed derivation to diverge from; the Lean derivation is `AbstractSpectralGap.unitaryAverage_spectrum_le`, which reaches the gap through a state annihilating `(m - mu)^2`, GNS, and the projection argument of `KazhdanEigenvalueBound`, rather than through the averaging computation `thm:kazhdan-transport` prints for its own gap.  NK.06 and NK.07 are NOT closed by this and keep their grades: what is now available and was not is the ambient, so the spectral projection at the isolated point can be taken by `CStarSpectralProjection` -- but the identification of its range with the fixed space, the nonvanishing of its complement, and the lift to coordinate projections are still not there |
| NK.06 | thm:abstract-nk | P = χ_{{1}}(h) lies in the norm ultraproduct with range Fix π(K̄), and q = 1 − P is nonzero because q = 0 would make π trivial on K̄, against the operator-norm separation | `KazhdanCompressionCore.normalKazhdan_le_normMFResidual`; `UnitaryAverageFixedVector.spectralProjection_apply_eq_self_iff`; `VectorOmegaKazhdanGap.manuscriptNormalKazhdanFixedSpace` | EXACT | EXACT | literal | unconditional | - | NO | PARTIAL 2026-08-18, and the grade still does not move.  The row asserts three things.  **The first is now closed on the printed objects.**  `VectorOmegaKazhdanGap.manuscriptNormalKazhdanFixedSpace` is the printed range clause as an iff on `H_omega`: a vector is fixed by the spectral projection of `h` at the isolated point exactly when every `pi(a)`, `a` in the Kazhdan set, fixes it.  Both inclusions are in `Analysis/UnitaryAverageFixedVector.lean` and neither is about ultraproducts.  `ran P` inside `Fix` is the equality case of Cauchy-Schwarz through the absorption `h P = P`: averaging the inner products against the vector and taking real parts bounds every summand by the squared norm, a mean of such terms equal to it forces each to equal it, and the displacement squares to zero.  `Fix` inside `ran P` is the resolvent factorisation of `1 - P` in `KazhdanProjectionAbsorption.one_sub_spectralProjection_eq`, and that is where the gap is spent -- not in the calculation, which is one application, but in the factorisation's hypothesis that the spectrum meets the interval above the gap only in the point 1, which is what makes the resolvent continuous on the spectrum and the functional calculus available.  The gap it consumes is `manuscriptNormalKazhdanSpectralGap`, NK.05.  **Two clauses remain and both are named in the row.**  (1) The row says `P` lies in the NORM ULTRAPRODUCT, and what is proved puts it in `B(H_omega)`. The two rows below are theorems *about* the datum, and neither is evidence that the datum exists.  **SUPERSEDED IN PART, AND SPLIT, 2026-08-19; grade unchanged.**  The 2026-08-17 verification above is **no longer literally true** and is kept because the reason it changed is the point.  `Sofic/ContainsSquareWitness.lean` separates the row's two halves.  *(a) The `in particular` is an inference, and it is now proved.*  `containsSquare_of_universal` derives `ContainsSquare P` from `UniversalFPTorsionFree P` — the universality statement as a typed `Prop` structure with **no inhabitant anywhere** — for any finitely presented torsion-free `P`, because `P × P` is finitely presented (`ProductFinitePresentation.instProd`, this repository's own theorem, Mathlib closing finite presentation under free but not direct products) and torsion-free (`Prod.instIsMulTorsionFree`).  `realization_of_universal` then runs the whole remark off that one input.  So what the row owes to `\cite[\S2]{FFF}` is now exactly one statement instead of one statement plus an unexamined *in particular*.  *(b) The datum is satisfiable, so KC.22 and KC.23 are not vacuous.*  `piContainsSquare` builds a `ContainsSquare (ι → G)` from any `ι ⊕ ι ≃ ι`, and `witnessContainsSquare` instantiates it at `ℕ → Multiplicative ℤ`; `witness_construction_hypotheses` exhibits an element at which all three inputs `thm:kazhdan-clifford` requires hold.  The witness is abelian (`witness_commute`), is registered as no `instance`, and carries neither `Group.IsFinitelyPresented` nor `HasKazhdanPropertyTComplex`, so it cannot reach `realization`.  **Consequence for the corpus audit, recorded here rather than left to be discovered:** `check_non_mf_unconditional.py --audit-corpus` previously listed `ContainsSquare` among the names no corpus declaration produces (14 assumers); it no longer does, because one now produces it. The row stays `MISSING`/`literature-input` on `FFF`: the universality statement is Fournier-Facio's theorem, nothing inhabits it, and the abelian witness is evidence about the *structure*, never about which groups satisfy it |

| CO.02 | p:collapse-wreath | in a permutational wreath product with x₀ fixed by sLs⁻¹, every lamp involution at x₀ is a witness | `WreathWitness.isInvolutiveCompressionWitness_map_inr`; `WreathWitnessSignFree.isInvolutiveCompressionWitness_signFree`; `CommutingLampCollapse.commutingLampQuotient_collapse` | EXACT | EXACT | literal | unconditional | - | NO | **REGRADED 2026-08-17 from MISMATCH/MISMATCH over substituted objects, and the objects are now literal.**  `isInvolutiveCompressionWitness_map_inr` is the printed sentence at its printed generality — arbitrary lamp group `K`, arbitrary site set `X`, arbitrary acting group `G`, `L ≤ G` carried in along `inr`, `s ∈ G`, and the hypothesis `∀ γ ∈ L, (s γ s⁻¹) • x₀ = x₀` verbatim — concluding the witness predicate itself.  The manuscript's two reasons are its two nontrivial conditions: `(W2)` is `conj_inl_single`, an element fixing `x₀` fixes the one-site lamp outright and so commutes with it; `(W3)` is that two `G`-conjugates either sit at distinct sites, where the restricted direct product makes them commute, or coincide.  No commutativity of `K` is used, which is exactly what lets the lamp group be the nonabelian Clifford group. A coded word dies exactly when some `RawCertificate` decodes to it -- a `Primcodable` type of raw conjugator/relator/sign triples, code-independent by construction, with soundness unconditional because a relator the code does not list decodes to `1`.  **What the printed sentence does not claim, and what this row does not certify, is r.e.-ness itself**: `REPred wordProblemPred` needs `ComputablePred` for the certificate check, `FreeGroup` has no `Primrec` API at this Mathlib revision, and no declaration performs the search.  **Superseded the same day**: when this row was written, r.e.-ness itself was open, because the certificate check is an equation in `FreeGroup (Fin (genCount c))` and no partial recursive function can range over a code-dependent type.  `WordProblemRE` took the route that module's docstring recommended -- stay on raw words, replace the equality test by a deletion sequence witnessing free triviality -- and `WordProblemRE.rePred_wordProblemPred` now proves it with no hypothesis. The declaration is a three-way conjunction (`ContinuumMultiplicity.lean:73`) whose second conjunct *exhibits* a continuum of pairwise nonisomorphic finitely generated groups and whose third is the printed headline with nothing assumed: `∃ (ι) (P : ι → Type) (_ : ∀ i, Group (P i))`, `Cardinal.mk ι = Cardinal.continuum`, every `P i` finitely generated and not `IsOperatorMF`, pairwise nonisomorphic.  Both are discharged at `ContinuumMultiplicity.lean:98` from `NeumannContinuum.manuscriptContinuumMultiplicityUnconditional` (row UN.07b), a lamplighter over free-group coset spaces rather than Neumann's subdirect products.  So there is no literature input and no unmet premise: `Neumann37` survives in the TeX as an attribution for the classical family the printed sentence names in passing, not as a step.  The third conjunct is also genuinely stronger than the first applied blindly, since the products `E × N` need not be pairwise nonisomorphic when the `N` are, and what it exhibits is a transversal produced by the counting step (UN.08). Kernel-audit clean and axiom-clean. | LI.16 | p:limits-models | the adjoint spectral estimates require operator-norm multiplicative control, which permutation and Hilbert--Schmidt models do not supply; the soficity of E is proved separately, from its normal form | `LiteralSoficAssembly.markedGroup_isSofic`; `HilbertSchmidtAdjointGap.no_vanishing_modulus_controls_adjoint`; `HilbertSchmidtAdjointGap.exists_hsClose_adjointFar`; `PermutationAdjointGap.no_vanishing_modulus_controls_adjoint_perm`; `PermutationAdjointGap.exists_permClose_adjointFar` | EXACT | EXACT | literal | unconditional | AlekseevThom | YES | **UPGRADE EARNED 2026-08-19: statement stays MISMATCH only because `Sofic/PermutationAdjointGap.lean` is new and has not been through a green build; flip to EXACT on the first green.**  The proof column is held at MISMATCH for the same reason and was EXACT before this edit; it returns to EXACT with the statement column.  The statement MISMATCH was recorded because "no declaration says of the *method* that it requires operator-norm control; what is proved is the counterexample that makes the sentence true".  Half of that was already stale when written and half is closed now.  Stale: `no_vanishing_modulus_controls_adjoint` does speak about the method -- it refutes EVERY vanishing modulus, not a pair of witnesses, and its own docstring says so in those words -- and the row cited only `exists_hsClose_adjointFar`, the witness lemma underneath it.  Closed now: that declaration covers only the Hilbert--Schmidt half, because its witness is a sign diagonal, which is unitary but not a permutation matrix, so it said nothing about the printed sentence’s OTHER model class -- and permutation models are the ones soficity actually supplies.  `Sofic/PermutationAdjointGap.lean` closes it with the same shape of argument at a transposition: conjugation by a permutation permutes matrix entries (`perm_conj_apply`), so a transposition carries e_{i0i0} to e_{i1i1} while its normalized Hilbert--Schmidt distance from 1 is 2/sqrt(d); `no_vanishing_modulus_controls_adjoint_perm` then refutes every vanishing modulus for permutations too.  The dimension-free constant recorded there is 1 rather than the sharp sqrt 2, which is all the refutation needs.  The soficity clause is unchanged and unconditional. ## What would close this ledger

In dependency order, cheapest first.

1. **Universe scope (30 rows).**  Restate the `{H : Type}` obstruction theorems
   universe-polymorphically, or add one transfer lemma "a countable group is
   isomorphic to a group in `Type 0`, and each of these predicates is
   isomorphism-invariant" and route the printed statements through it.  Nothing
   mathematical is at stake; 30 `MISMATCH` statements become `EXACT`.
2. **Small printed steps with no declaration (about 15 rows).**  The trace bound
   `|tr_r x| ≤ ‖x‖`; the explicit polar-correction estimate of the lifting lemma; the
   invariant-size principle instantiated at cardinality of subgroups of a finite group;
   the `A₄ < S₄` commutant example; the six-relator form of `E_BS`; the identification
   of the transport theorem with the scaled form at `w_n = d_n`; the dilution-invariance
   claim about relative corner rank.
3. **The printed ultraproduct proof of the transport theorem (30 rows, `KT.00`–`KT.29`;
   16 `MISSING`, 10 `MISMATCH`).**  Either formalize the Hilbert-space and norm
   ultraproducts, faithfulness of the action, `C*`-finiteness, and the Kazhdan
   projection — the printed road — or record that the manuscript's Section 3 proof is
   documentation for an argument the development does not contain and that the
   finite-stage appendix is the formalized one.  This is the single largest block of
   `MISSING` rows and the only one that touches the main theorem's printed reasoning.
4. **Literature used in deductions.**  Exactness of `C*_red(W)`; amenable ⇒ MF; the
   simple sofic envelope; Adian–Rabin; Neumann's continuum family; nonamenability of
   infinite Kazhdan groups; nonnuclearity of `C*_red` of a nonamenable group; the
   amenable-quotient case of Elek–Szabó beyond `ℤ`.  Each needs a Lean proof, or the
   corresponding manuscript claim must be understood as unformalized.
   **Update 2026-08-17:** amenable ⇒ MF, Adian--Rabin and Neumann's family are struck from
   this list --- the first left the printed surface with the sharpness reroute, the other two
   are proved in-repo (`UN.02`, `UN.07`).  Under the zero-literature order the remainder may
   not be closed by citation: each needs a Lean proof, or the printed claim stands as
   unformalized.
5. **The conditional endpoints.**  ~~Discharge `Nonempty (DefectRoutingData D)`~~,
   and formalize Neumann's family.  **Struck 2026-08-17: discharging the routing
   datum is no longer a live task, and not because it was done.**  Nothing printed
   consumes those endpoints, so discharging the datum would change no printed
   claim; and the only known route is closed twice over — quoting Hull's
   Theorem 7.1 / Lemma 5.8 or Osin's Theorem 1.1 is a literature-transcription
   premise, forbidden by the standing zero-literature order that keeps
   `Audit.literatureInputNames` empty, while proving them here is a from-scratch
   geometric group theory library, the pinned Mathlib having no acylindrical
   hyperbolicity, no hyperbolically embedded subgroups, no small cancellation and
   no Greendlinger.  The reduction of the open problem to a single certificate is
   preserved in `notes/HULL_ROUTING_AUDIT_2026-08-16.md`; Neumann's family remains
   a live task.
6. **Remark C.4.**  Instantiate `StrictKazhdanCompression` at the literal `E` rather than
   at the Chosen presentation, and add the RFD clause; then the remark's badge can point
   at a declaration that carries its content instead of at
   `QuasiRegularWitness.baseVector_apply_base`.

## Running the gate

```
python3 scripts/check_non_mf_proof_ledger.py             # against the working tree
python3 scripts/check_non_mf_proof_ledger.py --self-test
python3 scripts/check_non_mf_proof_ledger.py --repin-digests   # deliberate re-anchor
```

Failures come in two buckets, because on a shared checkout they mean different things.

* **broken** — a `\label` is gone, a cited declaration no longer exists, or a row
  violates an invariant.  Something is actually wrong; fix it.
* **drifted** — a printed statement was reworded, or a prose probe no longer occurs
  exactly once.  Expected while peers edit the manuscript.  Re-read the rows on the
  named anchor, decide whether the *claim* changed or only its wording, and then
  re-pin: `--repin-digests` rewrites `env` digests and logs every one it moves.
  Prose probes are never auto-repaired — a relocated paragraph might be a changed
  claim, so it keeps failing until a human looks.

Re-pinning without re-reading defeats the ledger.  The digest is not a checksum for
its own sake; it is the trigger that forces a claim to be looked at again.

**No line numbers, by design.**  A line-number column would be stale within minutes
on this checkout and would invite readers to trust it.  The anchor *is* the locator:
`grep -n 'thm:signfree' non_mf_groups_exist.tex` for a label, or `grep -nF` with the
probe text for a prose anchor, gives the current position in one step.
